[00:05:13] afternoon. Tenants and Workers United is a grassroots organization committed to building community power to win social and economic justice across northern Virginia. For over 39 years, Tenants and Workers United has been organizing low-income communities of color to build power through community-led campaigns for affordable housing, health equity, education justice, police accountability, immigrants' rights, and more. Tenants and Workers United has advocated for the creation of the county's Office of Housing and Community Development, the Housing Trust Fund, and the Affordable Dwelling Ordinance.

[00:05:51] Tenants and Workers United does a yearly Summer Youth Institute program in Eastern Prince William County for middle school and high school youth with the goal to provide leadership development, educational workshops, and guidance to ensure student success. Tenants and Workers United also provides education and leadership development to help people become change agents, and proactive civic engagement to secure sustainable systemic changes that advance racial, economic, and social justice all across Prince William.

[00:06:24] Therefore, today, the Prince William Board of County Supervisors hereby recognizes and commends the outstanding efforts that the Tenants and Workers United has contributed to Prince William County residents. So, good afternoon, Chairwomen, Jefferson, and supervisors.

[00:08:54] right now. On the evening of November 13th, 2025, a major gas leak caused by a water main rupture in Lake Ridge necessitated the evacuation of 82 residents. At 9.39 p.m., Prince William County

[00:09:30] firefighters and rescue workers responded to the area near the intersection of Oakwood Drive and Cantonia Place to investigate reports of leaking gas and determined that a three-inch gas line was severely damaged during excavation work, putting residents of 65 townhomes in danger.

[00:09:47] The Office of Emergency Management coordinated with the Prince William Public Schools and Omni ride to provide immediate shelter at Woodbridge High School for 82 people, 11 of whom were children, with additional fire and rescue units providing evacuation and shelter support. [00:29:48] For example, the Arts Council has helped facilitate rehearsal and performance space, including discounted access to school facilities and community venues. [00:30:09] It would also limit our ability to provide free concerts for the community. My quintet has performed over 30 free concerts in the past few years, and that's in addition to the 15 to 20 concerts that the Prince William Community Band has put on.

[00:30:45] They happen because there is a central county-supported body making them happen. And Arts Alive is another example. This festival showcases everyone from small volunteer ensembles to large orchestras along with dance and visual arts groups from across the county.

[00:31:01] It takes months and months of planning and extensive volunteer coordination. The Arts Alive is a public asset built through long-term stewardship and turning it over abruptly to a private model risks disrupting continuity and eroding community trust.

[00:31:19] The Arts Council is also currently helping us plan a woodwind workshop for middle school and high school students, an educational opportunity that would not exist without their coordination. [01:38:35] This was done in ESRI's ArcGIS platform, online platform. And we have, first on the right, several things that tell you what the build-out analysis is, how to use the site, terms and definitions, some frequently asked questions that we'll build out as we get those questions in.

[01:39:29] The build-out analysis is in three buckets. I like to think of it in three buckets. We have the existing information, existing dwellings construction. We have the zone but not built yet.

[01:39:44] So this is something that has zoning approval. We know how many units or approximately how many units or how many square feet are going in for commercial or retail or industrial uses. And so this is a list of cases.

[01:40:00] We call it sometimes the residential inventory, the non-residential inventory. So that's the zone but not built yet. And then the third bucket is things that are planned but not zoned yet. So the comprehensive plan is a guide for growth and development, and it has areas that we're looking for redevelopment or new development that haven't been zoned yet to the appropriate zoning.

[01:40:23] So those are the three buckets. And as something gets rezoned from bucket one, the planned and not zoned, It automatically is going to show up in the zone but not built. And then once it's built, it will be quarterly updated with that information.

[01:40:43] So we can see off the bat that there's 160,000, 170 existing residential dwelling units in the county right now based on our occupancy reports. There are a potential remaining to be built, based on the comprehensive plan, 54,542 more units.

[05:12:24] But instead, it was left out, and it was up-planned from SRR for development. So as a result, we're now facing a proposal that would pretty much clear-cut nearly 100% of an intact forest in the watershed that supplies the drinking water to up to a million residents.

[05:12:46] And so we just keep seeing this erosion across the county. So second, this parcel is located in what PWCA is referring to as the forest belt. And actually, if I can get this little doohickey here, we can see it together.

[05:13:35] I'm sure I'm leaving Rumsco Creek. But then also the Orpa area in the reservoir. So, and then the red areas. So this proposal represents a further outward expansion of our suburban urban footprint.

[05:13:54] So those red areas on the map into the landscapes that play a critical role in protecting our waterways. And each of these decisions, taking individually, may seem small, but collectively they add up to a steady erosion of our remaining forest. So from a smart growth perspective, we would prefer to see this type of development in maybe even higher density directed to those red areas and areas where infrastructure already exists.

[05:14:21] And we can build a critical mass needed to support existing public transit hubs and walkable communities. rather than continuing to push development outward, which undermines those goals. [05:14:50] So as long as we fail meaningful incentives for redevelopment or incentivized conservation, This is the type of things that we'll be continuing to see. And so I guess I want to leave with, like, what if we had a goal of maintaining our existing tree cover and really took that seriously?

[06:28:43] committed to construct a sediment basin at the very beginning of the development pre-construction, And essentially what this does is it allows for there to be additional mitigation treatment during construction.
